| 36 | 79.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 137. Controls at paragraph 136 shall be suitably factored in the contract agreement |

| 36 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | signed with the third-party Switch ASPs. These controls shall be applicable to the |

| 36 | 120.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | ASPs limited to the IT ecosystem (such as physical infrastructure, hardware, |

| 36 | 141.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | software, reconciliation system, network interfaces, security solutions, hardware |

| 36 | 161.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | security module, middleware, associated people, processes, systems, data, and |

| 36 | 182.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | information) providing ATM switch services as well as any other type of payment |

| 36 | 203.3 | 100.4 | 291.8 | system related services to the bank. |

| 36 | 236.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 138. The bank shall ensure to mandate the following baseline cybersecurity controls |

| 36 | 256.7 | 100.4 | 358.4 | in their contractual agreements with these ASPs: |

| 36 | 289.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (1) The default passwords of all the network devices / systems of the ASP shall |

| 36 | 310.1 | 128.7 | 282.1 | be changed after installation. |

| 36 | 342.8 | 100.3 | 425.3 | (2) The ASP shall not use trivial and / or default passwords. |

| 36 | 375.5 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (3) The ASP shall implement a centralised authentication and authorisation |

| 36 | 396.2 | 128.7 | 523.3 | system through an Identity and Access Management solution for accessing |

| 36 | 416.9 | 128.7 | 523.3 | and administering applications, operating systems, databases, network and |

| 36 | 437.6 | 128.7 | 523.3 | security devices / systems, point of connectivity (local / remote) including |

| 36 | 458.3 | 128.7 | 523.3 | enforcement of strong password policy, two-factor / multi-factor |

| 36 | 479.0 | 128.7 | 523.3 | authentication depending on risk assessment, securing privileged accesses |

| 36 | 499.7 | 128.7 | 474.3 | following the principle of least privileges and separation of duties. |

| 36 | 532.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (4) The ASP shall ensure to provide access to critical servers, network and |

| 36 | 553.0 | 128.7 | 523.4 | security devices / systems through Privileged User Management Systems / |

| 36 | 573.7 | 128.7 | 388.8 | Identity and Access Management (IAM) systems. |

| 36 | 606.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (5) The ASP shall design the infrastructure with adequate network segregation |

| 36 | 627.1 | 128.7 | 174.0 | controls. |

| 36 | 659.8 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (6) The ASP shall define firewall rules to block unidentified outbound |

| 36 | 680.5 | 128.7 | 523.2 | connections, reverse Transmission Control Protocol (TCP) shells, and other |

| 36 | 701.2 | 128.7 | 297.4 | potential backdoor connections. |

| 36 | 733.9 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (7) The ASP shall disable remote connections from outside machines to the |

| 36 | 754.6 | 128.7 | 375.2 | network hosting the ATM Switch infrastructure. |

| 36 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 36 |

| 37 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 37 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 37 | 79.1 | 100.3 | 523.4 | (8) The ASP shall enable Internet Protocol (IP) table to restrict access to the |

| 37 | 99.8 | 128.7 | 523.3 | clients and servers in Society for Worldwide Interbank Financial |

| 37 | 120.5 | 128.7 | 523.3 | Telecommunication (SWIFT) and ATM Switch environment only to |

| 37 | 141.2 | 128.7 | 235.5 | authorised systems. |

| 37 | 173.9 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (9) The ASP shall ensure software integrity of the ATM Switch related |

| 37 | 194.6 | 128.7 | 195.4 | applications. |

| 37 | 227.3 | 100.3 | 523.2 | (10) The ASP shall ensure to appropriately mask the data used for development |

| 37 | 248.0 | 128.7 | 190.7 | and testing. |

| 37 | 280.7 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (11) The ASP shall certify any new products and updates, upgrades as having |

| 37 | 301.4 | 128.7 | 523.3 | been developed following secure coding practices. The application |

| 37 | 322.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | architecture shall be tested to safeguard the confidentiality and integrity of |

| 37 | 342.8 | 128.7 | 523.4 | data being stored, processed, and transmitted. An assurance to this effect |

| 37 | 363.5 | 128.7 | 445.6 | shall be shared with the bank / RBI as and when requested. |

| 37 | 396.2 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (12) In respect of critical business applications, the ASP shall conduct source |

| 37 | 416.9 | 128.7 | 523.2 | code audits by professionally competent personnel / service providers. |

| 37 | 437.6 | 128.7 | 523.4 | They shall provide assurance to the bank that the application is free from |

| 37 | 458.3 | 128.7 | 334.8 | embedded malicious / fraudulent code. |

| 37 | 491.0 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (13) The ASP shall have a robust change management process in place to |

| 37 | 511.7 | 128.7 | 523.3 | record / monitor all the changes that are moved / pushed into the production |

| 37 | 532.4 | 128.7 | 523.3 | environment. Such a change management process must clearly mention |

| 37 | 553.0 | 128.7 | 523.3 | the test cases, chain of approving authority for the particular change, |

| 37 | 573.7 | 128.7 | 317.5 | deployment plan, and rollback plan. |

| 37 | 606.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (14) The ASP shall put in place a mechanism to monitor the database security |

| 37 | 627.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | events, backend access to the databases to ensure access to the database |

| 37 | 647.8 | 128.7 | 523.3 | is restricted and the activities carried out through the backend are logged |

| 37 | 668.5 | 128.7 | 203.4 | and reviewed. |

| 37 | 701.2 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (15) The ASP shall ensure to generate and capture logs from devices / |

| 37 | 721.9 | 128.7 | 260.7 | applications / databases. |

| 37 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 37 |

| 38 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 38 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 38 | 79.1 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (16) The ASP shall ensure to capture audit logs pertaining to user actions in a |

| 38 | 99.8 | 128.7 | 506.9 | system. Such arrangements shall facilitate forensic auditing, if need be. |

| 38 | 132.5 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (17) The ASP shall implement an alert mechanism to monitor any change in the |

| 38 | 153.2 | 128.7 | 192.8 | log settings. |

| 38 | 185.9 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (18) The ASP is responsible for meeting the requirements prescribed for incident |

| 38 | 206.6 | 128.7 | 523.6 | management and Business Continuity Plan (BCP) / DR even if their IT |

| 38 | 227.3 | 128.7 | 523.4 | infrastructure, systems, and applications, are managed by third-party |

| 38 | 248.0 | 128.7 | 523.3 | vendors / service providers. The ASP shall have necessary arrangements, |

| 38 | 268.7 | 128.7 | 523.2 | including a documented procedure for such purpose. This shall include, |

| 38 | 289.4 | 128.7 | 523.3 | among other things, informing the bank about any cyber incident occurring |

| 38 | 310.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | in respect of the bank on a timely basis to mitigate the risk at the earliest as |

| 38 | 330.8 | 128.7 | 378.1 | well as to meet extant regulatory requirements. |

| 38 | 363.5 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (19) The ASP shall periodically conduct Vulnerability Assessment / Penetration |

| 38 | 384.2 | 128.7 | 488.0 | Testing (VA / PT) of applications, servers, and network components. |

| 38 | 416.9 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (20) The ASP shall ensure to share the VA / PT report(s) and compliance to its |

| 38 | 437.6 | 128.7 | 405.5 | findings with the bank / RBI as and when requested. |

| 38 | 470.3 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (21) The ASP’s risk management / treatment framework shall ensure that the |

| 38 | 491.0 | 128.7 | 523.2 | vulnerabilities detected are remediated promptly to avoid exploitation of |

| 38 | 511.7 | 128.7 | 233.4 | such vulnerabilities. |

| 38 | 544.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (22) The ASP shall have a mechanism and resources to take appropriate action |

| 38 | 565.0 | 128.7 | 523.2 | in case of any cyber incident. The ASP shall have a written incident |

| 38 | 585.7 | 128.7 | 523.3 | response procedure including the roles of staff / outsourced staff handling |

| 38 | 606.4 | 128.7 | 523.3 | such incidents. The response strategies shall consider readiness to meet |

| 38 | 627.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | various incident scenarios based on situational awareness, potential / post |

| 38 | 647.8 | 128.7 | 523.2 | impact, and consistent communication and co-ordination with stakeholders, |

| 38 | 668.5 | 128.7 | 356.7 | specifically with the bank, during response. |

| 38 | 701.2 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (23) The ASP shall set up a Cyber Security Operations Centre (CSOC). The |

| 38 | 721.9 | 128.7 | 357.6 | CSOC, among other things, shall ensure to |

| 38 | 754.6 | 128.7 | 456.3 | (i) seamlessly collect the logs relevant to the IT ecosystem; |

| 39 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 39 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 39 | 79.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | (ii) store, process, and correlate the logs through appropriate Security |

| 39 | 99.8 | 157.1 | 523.3 | Information and Event Management (SIEM) solution for continuous |

| 39 | 120.5 | 157.1 | 247.2 | surveillance; and |

| 39 | 153.2 | 128.7 | 492.0 | (iii) regularly update on the latest nature of emerging cyber threats. |

| 39 | 227.3 | 128.7 | 523.6 | Software Security Framework (PCI-SSF), as applicable to the IT |

| 39 | 248.0 | 128.7 | 189.4 | ecosystem. |

| 39 | 322.1 | 100.4 | 292.5 | the ASPs for necessary compliance. |

| 39 | 355.4 | 72.0 | 235.1 | R. Cryptographic Controls |

| 39 | 387.5 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 140. The key length, algorithms, cipher suites and applicable protocols used in |

| 39 | 408.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | transmission channels, processing of data and authentication purpose shall be |

| 39 | 428.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | strong. The bank shall adopt internationally accepted and published standards |

| 39 | 449.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | that are not deprecated / demonstrated to be insecure / vulnerable, and the |

| 39 | 470.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | configurations involved in implementing such controls shall be compliant with |

| 39 | 491.0 | 100.4 | 307.8 | extant laws and regulatory instructions. |

| 39 | 524.3 | 72.0 | 265.6 | S. Straight Through Processing |

| 39 | 556.4 | 72.0 | 523.2 | 141. In order to prevent unauthorised modification of data, the bank shall ensure that |

| 39 | 577.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | there is no manual intervention or manual modification in data while it is being |

| 39 | 597.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | transferred from one process to another or from one application to another, in |

| 39 | 618.4 | 100.4 | 260.4 | respect of critical applications. |

| 39 | 651.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 142. Data transfer mechanism between processes or applications shall be properly |

| 39 | 671.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | tested, securely automated with necessary checks and balances, and properly |

| 39 | 692.5 | 100.4 | 523.4 | integrated through ‘Straight Through Processing’ methodology with appropriate |

| 39 | 713.2 | 100.4 | 324.5 | authentication mechanism and audit trails. |

| 39 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 39 |

| 40 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 40 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 40 | 79.7 | 72.0 | 241.1 | T. Continuous Surveillance |

| 40 | 111.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 143. The bank shall set up a CSOC to ensure continuous surveillance and keep itself |

| 40 | 132.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | regularly updated on the latest nature of emerging cyber threats. An indicative, |

| 40 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | but not exhaustive, minimum baseline guidance on CSOC is given in Chapter VI |

| 40 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 203.1 | of these Directions. |

| 40 | 207.2 | 72.0 | 406.4 | U. Advanced Real-time Threat Defence and Management |

| 40 | 239.3 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 144. The bank shall build a robust defence against the installation, spread, and |

| 40 | 260.0 | 100.4 | 441.9 | execution of malicious code at multiple points in its environment. |

| 40 | 292.7 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 145. The bank shall implement anti-malware, antivirus protection including |

| 40 | 313.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | behavioural detection capabilities across all relevant devices and environments, |

| 40 | 334.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | such as endpoints (including PCs, laptops, and mobile devices), servers |

| 40 | 354.8 | 100.4 | 523.2 | (operating systems, databases, and applications), Web / Internet gateways, |

| 40 | 375.5 | 100.4 | 523.2 | email-gateways, Wireless networks, and Short Message Service (SMS) servers. |

| 40 | 396.2 | 100.4 | 523.4 | The bank shall also implement tools and processes for centralised management |

| 40 | 416.9 | 100.4 | 285.2 | and monitoring of these measures. |

| 40 | 449.6 | 72.0 | 463.1 | 146. The bank shall implement whitelisting of internet websites / systems. |

| 40 | 482.3 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 147. The bank shall consider implementing secure web gateways with capability to |

| 40 | 503.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | deep scan network packets including secure Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure |

| 40 | 523.7 | 100.4 | 414.7 | (HTTPS) traffic passing through the web / internet gateway. |

| 40 | 557.0 | 72.0 | 178.4 | V. Anti-Phishing |

| 40 | 589.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 148. The bank shall subscribe to anti-phishing / anti-rogue app services from external |

| 40 | 609.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | service providers for identifying and taking down phishing websites / rogue |

| 40 | 630.4 | 100.4 | 167.1 | applications. |

| 40 | 663.7 | 72.0 | 344.4 | W. Vulnerability Assessment and Penetration Test |

| 40 | 695.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 149. The bank shall conduct VA and PT exercises periodically for all the critical and |

| 40 | 715.7 | 100.4 | 226.4 | internet facing systems. |

| 40 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 40 |

| 41 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 41 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 41 | 79.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 150. The bank shall periodically conduct VA / PT of critical, internet facing web / mobile |

| 41 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | applications, servers, and network components throughout their lifecycle |

| 41 | 120.5 | 100.4 | 473.9 | including pre-implementation, post implementation, and after changes. |

| 41 | 153.2 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 151. For critical information systems and / or those in the DMZ having customer |

| 41 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | interface, VA shall be conducted at least once in every six months and PT at least |

| 41 | 194.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | once in 12 months. For non-critical information systems, a risk-based approach |

| 41 | 215.3 | 100.4 | 523.4 | shall be adopted to decide the requirement and periodicity of conduct of VA / PT. |

| 41 | 248.0 | 72.0 | 523.5 | 152. In the post implementation (of IT project / system upgrade) scenario, the VA / PT |

| 41 | 268.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | shall be performed on the production environment. Under unavoidable |

| 41 | 289.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | circumstances, if the PT is conducted in test environment, the bank shall ensure |

| 41 | 310.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | that the version and configuration of the test environment resembles the |

| 41 | 330.8 | 100.4 | 523.4 | production environment. Any deviation should be documented and approved by |

| 41 | 351.5 | 100.4 | 143.7 | the ISC. |

| 41 | 384.2 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 153. The bank shall ensure to fix the identified vulnerabilities and associated risks in |

| 41 | 404.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | a time-bound manner by undertaking requisite corrective measures and ensure |

| 41 | 425.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | that the compliance is sustained to avoid recurrence of known vulnerabilities |

| 41 | 446.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | such as those available in Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures (CVE) |

| 41 | 467.0 | 100.4 | 153.0 | database. |

| 41 | 499.7 | 72.0 | 523.5 | 154. The bank shall put in place a documented approach for conduct of VA / PT |

| 41 | 520.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | covering the scope, coverage, vulnerability scoring mechanism (e.g., Common |

| 41 | 541.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | Vulnerability Scoring System) and all other aspects. This shall also apply to the |

| 41 | 561.7 | 100.4 | 411.6 | bank’s information systems hosted in a cloud environment. |

| 41 | 594.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 155. VA / PT shall be conducted by appropriately trained and independent information |

| 41 | 615.1 | 100.4 | 240.5 | security experts / auditors. |

| 41 | 647.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 156. The bank shall have control / check over audit methodology, processes, and |

| 41 | 668.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | competence of auditors so as to ensure that audit objectives are fully met. At the |

| 41 | 689.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | time of selecting, appointing or engaging or renewing the contract with the VA / |

| 41 | 709.9 | 100.4 | 523.4 | PT auditor or consultant, the bank shall consider aspects such as requisite |

| 41 | 730.6 | 100.4 | 523.2 | qualification, professional expertise (of the firm or company as well as the audit |

| 41 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 41 |

| 42 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 42 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 42 | 79.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | personnel engaged by the entity), appropriate credentials, and suitable |

| 42 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 167.5 | competency. |

| 42 | 132.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 157. The bank shall review the coverage and scope of the VA / PT and ensure that |

| 42 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | reasonable assurance for each of the areas therein shall be provided explicitly in |

| 42 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | the VA / PT audit reports. The same shall be mentioned at the time of empanelling |

| 42 | 194.6 | 100.4 | 433.5 | or selecting and awarding the contract with the VA / PT auditor. |

| 42 | 227.3 | 72.0 | 523.5 | 158. The bank shall, on an ongoing basis, review the performance of the VA / PT |

| 42 | 248.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | auditor and ensure that appropriate action is taken in case of noting any |

| 42 | 268.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | deficiencies. For instance, systems, applications, or infrastructure that were |

| 42 | 289.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | subjected to VA / PT, ceteris paribus, if found to have been compromised at a |

| 42 | 310.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | later date, apparently due to vulnerabilities that were not observed or highlighted |

| 42 | 330.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | on timely basis in the VA / PT will qualify as a deficiency in discharge of function |

| 42 | 351.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | by the VA / PT auditor. Such deficiencies shall also be factored in while evaluating |

| 42 | 372.2 | 100.4 | 523.4 | the competency when selecting or renewing the contract with the VA / PT auditor. |

| 42 | 404.9 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 159. The bank shall continuously interact with the VA / PT auditors during the audit |

| 42 | 425.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | and in case of CERT-In empanelled auditors, the bank shall be guided by CERT- |

| 42 | 446.3 | 100.4 | 417.0 | In’s Comprehensive Cyber Security Audit Policy Guidelines. |

| 42 | 479.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 160. Findings of VA / PT and the follow up actions necessitated shall be monitored |

| 42 | 499.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | closely by the Information Security / Information Systems Audit team as well as |

| 42 | 520.4 | 100.4 | 211.8 | Senior Management. |

| 42 | 553.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 161. The status of the closure of the VA / PT observations shall be put to the ITSC |

| 42 | 573.7 | 100.4 | 297.2 | and ISC at least on a quarterly basis. |

| 42 | 607.0 | 72.0 | 235.1 | X. Red Teaming Exercises |

| 42 | 639.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 162. The bank may conduct red teaming exercises to identify the vulnerabilities and |

| 42 | 659.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | the business risk, assess the efficacy of the defences and check the mitigating |

| 42 | 680.5 | 100.4 | 523.2 | controls already in place by simulating the objectives and actions of an attacker. |

| 42 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 42 |

| 43 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 43 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 43 | 79.7 | 72.0 | 349.2 | Y. Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery |

| 43 | 111.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 163. The BCP and DR policy shall adopt best practices (e.g., ISO 22301) to guide its |

| 43 | 132.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | actions in reducing the likelihood or impact of disruptive incidents and |

| 43 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.4 | maintaining business continuity. The policy shall be updated based on major |

| 43 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 297.2 | developments and risk assessments. |

| 43 | 206.6 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 164. The bank’s BCP and DR capabilities shall be designed to effectively support |

| 43 | 227.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | resilience objectives and enable rapid recovery and secure resumption of critical |

| 43 | 248.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | operations including key cybersecurity controls post cyber-attacks / other |

| 43 | 268.7 | 100.4 | 523.4 | incidents aligned with recovery time objectives while ensuring security of |

| 43 | 289.4 | 100.4 | 208.4 | processes and data. |

| 43 | 322.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 165. Periodicity of DR drills for critical information systems shall be at least on a half- |

| 43 | 342.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | yearly basis and for other information systems, as per the bank’s risk |

| 43 | 363.5 | 100.4 | 167.7 | assessment. |

| 43 | 396.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 166. Any major issues observed during the DR drill shall be resolved and tested again |

| 43 | 416.9 | 100.4 | 407.9 | to ensure successful conduct of drill before the next cycle. |

| 43 | 449.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 167. The DR testing shall involve switching over to the DR / alternate site and thus |

| 43 | 470.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | using it as the primary site for sufficiently long period where usual business |

| 43 | 491.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | operations of at least a full working day (including Beginning of Day to End of |

| 43 | 511.7 | 100.4 | 255.1 | Day operations) are covered. |

| 43 | 544.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 168. The bank shall regularly test the BCP / DR under different scenarios for possible |

| 43 | 565.0 | 100.4 | 453.0 | types of contingencies, to ensure that it is up-to-date and effective. |

| 43 | 597.7 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 169. The bank shall backup data and periodically restore such backed-up data to |

| 43 | 618.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | check its usability. The integrity of such backup data shall be preserved along |

| 43 | 639.1 | 100.4 | 323.8 | with securing it from unauthorised access. |

| 43 | 671.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 170. The bank shall ensure that DR architecture and procedures are robust, meeting |

| 43 | 692.5 | 100.4 | 523.4 | the defined Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and Recovery Point Objective (RPO) |

| 43 | 713.2 | 100.4 | 370.3 | for any recovery operations in case of contingency. |

| 43 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 43 |

| 44 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 44 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 44 | 79.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 171. The bank shall prioritise achieving minimal RTO (as approved by the bank’s |

| 44 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 416.5 | ITSC) and a near zero RPO for critical information systems. |

| 44 | 132.5 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 172. In a scenario of non-zero RPO, the bank shall have a documented methodology |

| 44 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 516.0 | for reconciliation of data while resuming operations from the alternate location. |

| 44 | 185.9 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 173. The bank shall ensure that the configurations of information systems and |

| 44 | 206.6 | 100.4 | 408.5 | deployed security patches at the DC and DR are identical. |

| 44 | 239.3 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 174. The bank shall ensure BCP and DR capabilities on critical interconnected |

| 44 | 260.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | systems and networks including those of vendors and partners. The bank shall |

| 44 | 280.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | ensure demonstrated readiness through collaborative and co-ordinated |

| 44 | 301.4 | 100.4 | 338.5 | resilience testing that meets the bank’s RTO. |

| 44 | 334.7 | 72.0 | 406.4 | Z. Cyber Incident Response and Recovery Management |

| 44 | 367.4 | 72.0 | 280.6 | Z.1 Responding to Cyber-Incidents |

| 44 | 399.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 175. The bank shall put in place cyber incident response and recovery management |

| 44 | 420.2 | 100.4 | 439.2 | policy and related procedures which shall address the following: |

| 44 | 452.9 | 100.3 | 412.8 | (1) definition, classification, and assessment of incidents; |

| 44 | 485.6 | 100.3 | 523.4 | (2) allocation of roles and responsibilities of staff as well as outsourced staff |

| 44 | 506.3 | 128.7 | 312.8 | manning / handling such incidents; |

| 44 | 539.0 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (3) mechanism for reporting of cyber incidents by employees, vendors, and |

| 44 | 559.7 | 128.7 | 187.4 | customers; |

| 44 | 592.4 | 100.3 | 476.9 | (4) clear communication strategy and plan to manage such incidents; |

| 44 | 625.0 | 100.3 | 450.8 | (5) measures to contain exposures and achieve timely recovery; |

| 44 | 657.7 | 100.3 | 341.4 | (6) collection / sharing of threat information; |

| 44 | 690.4 | 100.3 | 260.8 | (7) post incident review; and |

| 44 | 723.1 | 100.3 | 364.7 | (8) periodical testing of incident response plans. |

| 44 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 44 |

| 45 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 45 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 45 | 79.1 | 72.0 | 523.2 | 176. The bank shall provide specialised training to the personnel handling cyber |

| 45 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 151.1 | incidents. |

| 45 | 132.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 177. Response plans shall consider readiness to meet various incident scenarios |

| 45 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.2 | based on situational awareness, potential / post impact, and consistent |

| 45 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 465.9 | communication and co-ordination with stakeholders during response. |

| 45 | 206.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 178. The bank shall have mechanism to dynamically incorporate lessons learnt to |

| 45 | 227.3 | 100.4 | 311.2 | continually improve the response plans. |

| 45 | 260.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 179. The bank shall document and communicate strategies to respond to advanced |

| 45 | 280.7 | 100.4 | 498.5 | attacks such as ransomware / cyber extortion, data destruction, and DDoS. |

| 45 | 313.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 180. The bank shall contain the level of cyber-attack by implementing shielding |

| 45 | 334.1 | 100.4 | 385.7 | controls / quarantining the affected devices / systems. |

| 45 | 366.8 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 181. The bank shall analyse cyber incidents (including through forensic analysis, if |

| 45 | 387.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | necessary) for their severity, impact, and root cause. The bank shall take |

| 45 | 408.2 | 100.4 | 523.4 | measures, corrective and preventive, to mitigate the adverse impact of incidents |

| 45 | 428.9 | 100.4 | 227.1 | on business operations. |

| 45 | 461.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 182. The bank shall report cyber incidents within six hours of detection on DAKSH |

| 45 | 482.3 | 100.4 | 523.4 | platform (Reserve Bank’s Advanced Supervisory Monitoring System - |

| 45 | 503.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | https://daksh.rbi.org.in). The bank shall also pro-actively notify CERT-In |

| 45 | 523.7 | 100.4 | 237.8 | regarding cyber incidents. |

| 45 | 556.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 183. The bank shall have clear communication plans for escalation and reporting the |

| 45 | 577.0 | 100.4 | 523.4 | incidents to the Board and Senior Management as well as to customers, as |

| 45 | 597.7 | 100.4 | 147.7 | required. |

| 45 | 630.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 184. The bank may actively participate in the activities of CISO’s forum coordinated |

| 45 | 651.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | by IDRBT and share the threat intelligence arising from cyber incidents to Indian |

| 45 | 671.8 | 100.4 | 504.0 | Banks-Centre for Analysis of Risks and Threats (IB-CART) set up by IDRBT. |

| 45 | 704.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 185. The bank shall establish appropriate arrangements for external coordination and |

| 45 | 725.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | information sharing, including integration of threat intelligence and security |

| 45 | 745.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | information feeds from relevant internal and external sources, and coordination |

| 45 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 45 |

| 46 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 46 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 46 | 79.1 | 100.4 | 523.4 | with industry participants, cyber response agencies, CERT-In, telecom service |

| 46 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 391.2 | providers, and other stakeholders, as may be required. |

| 46 | 132.5 | 72.0 | 523.2 | 186. The bank shall establish and implement systems to collect and share threat |

| 46 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | information from local / national / international sources following legally |

| 46 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 293.1 | accepted / defined means / process. |

| 46 | 207.2 | 72.0 | 276.7 | Z.2 Recovery from Cyber Incidents |

| 46 | 239.3 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 187. The bank shall establish processes to improve incident response and recovery |

| 46 | 260.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | activities and capabilities through lessons learnt from past incidents as well as |

| 46 | 280.7 | 100.4 | 286.4 | from the conduct of tests and drills. |

| 46 | 313.4 | 72.0 | 525.6 | 188. The bank shall periodically and actively participate in cyber drills and related |

| 46 | 334.1 | 100.4 | 525.6 | simulation exercises conducted under the aegis of institutions / agencies such as |

| 46 | 354.8 | 100.4 | 203.7 | Cert-IN and IDRBT. |

| 46 | 387.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 189. The bank shall ensure resilient capabilities in all interconnected systems and |

| 46 | 408.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | networks including those of vendors and partners and readiness demonstrated |

| 46 | 428.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | through collaborative and coordinated resilience testing that meets the bank’s |

| 46 | 449.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | recovery time objectives. Adequate capacity shall be planned and maintained, in |

| 46 | 470.3 | 100.4 | 215.8 | consideration thereof. |

| 46 | 503.0 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 190. The bank, inter alia, shall ensure effectiveness of crisis communication plan / |

| 46 | 523.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | process by conduct of periodic drills / testing with stakeholders (including |

| 46 | 544.4 | 100.4 | 280.5 | customers and service providers). |

| 46 | 577.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 191. The bank shall align Security Operation Centre, Incident Response and Digital |

| 46 | 597.7 | 100.4 | 494.0 | forensics to reduce the business downtime and restore normal operations. |

| 46 | 631.0 | 72.0 | 275.4 | Z.3 Cyber Crisis Management Plan |

| 46 | 663.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 192. The bank shall document a Cyber Crisis Management Plan (CCMP) which shall |

| 46 | 683.8 | 100.4 | 447.0 | be part of the overall Board approved framework in cybersecurity. |

| 46 | 716.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 193. CCMP shall address the following four aspects: (i) Detection, (ii) Containment, |

| 46 | 737.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | (iii) Response, and (iv) Recovery. The bank shall take effective measures to |

| 46 | 757.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | prevent cyber-attacks and to promptly detect any cyber-intrusions so as to |

| 46 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 46 |

| 47 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 47 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 47 | 79.1 | 100.4 | 523.4 | contain / respond / recover from the fallout. The bank shall be well prepared to |

| 47 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 523.4 | face emerging cyber-threats such as ‘zero-day’ attacks, remote access threats, |

| 47 | 120.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | and targeted attacks. Among other things, the bank shall take necessary |

| 47 | 141.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | preventive and corrective measures in addressing various types of cyber threats |

| 47 | 161.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | including, but not limited to, denial of service, DDoS, ransomware, destructive |

| 47 | 182.6 | 100.4 | 523.4 | malware, business email frauds including spam, email phishing, spear phishing, |

| 47 | 203.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | whaling, vishing frauds, drive-by downloads, browser gateway fraud, ghost |

| 47 | 224.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | administrator exploits, identity frauds, memory update frauds, and password |

| 47 | 244.7 | 100.4 | 177.1 | related frauds. |

| 47 | 278.0 | 72.0 | 142.5 | AA. Metrics |

| 47 | 310.1 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 194. The bank shall develop a comprehensive set of metrics that provide for |

| 47 | 330.8 | 100.4 | 523.4 | prospective and retrospective measures, like key performance indicators and key |

| 47 | 351.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | risk indicators. Some illustrative metrics include coverage of anti-malware |

| 47 | 372.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | software and their updation percentage, patch latency, extent of user awareness |

| 47 | 392.9 | 100.4 | 318.6 | training, and vulnerability related metrics. |

| 47 | 425.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 195. Additionally, the bank shall define suitable metrics for system performance, |

| 47 | 446.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | recovery, and business resumption, including RPO and RTO, for all critical |

| 47 | 467.0 | 100.4 | 523.2 | information systems. For non-critical information systems, the bank shall adopt |

| 47 | 487.7 | 100.4 | 358.5 | a risk-based approach to define suitable metrics. |

| 47 | 520.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 196. The bank shall implement suitable scorecard / metrics / methodology to measure |

| 47 | 541.0 | 100.4 | 299.3 | IT performance, and IT maturity level. |

| 47 | 573.7 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 197. The adequacy of and adherence to cybersecurity framework shall be assessed |

| 47 | 594.4 | 100.4 | 523.4 | and measured through development of indicators to assess the level of risk / |

| 47 | 615.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | preparedness. The awareness among the stakeholders including employees |

| 47 | 635.8 | 100.4 | 315.9 | may also form a part of this assessment. |

| 47 | 669.1 | 72.0 | 343.8 | BB. User / Employee / Management Awareness |

| 47 | 701.2 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 198. The bank shall proactively promote awareness of its cyber resilience objectives |

| 47 | 721.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | among service providers, partners, and other stakeholders, and shall require |

| 47 | 742.6 | 100.4 | 447.9 | appropriate actions to ensure aligned implementation and testing. |

| 47 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 47 |

| 48 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 48 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 48 | 79.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 199. The bank shall define and communicate its security policies to users / employees, |

| 48 | 99.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | vendors, and partners, covering secure and acceptable use of bank’s information |

| 48 | 120.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | assets including customer information / data, and educating them about |

| 48 | 141.2 | 100.4 | 405.8 | cybersecurity risks and protection measures at their level. |

| 48 | 173.9 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 200. The bank shall provide targeted awareness and training for key personnel in |

| 48 | 194.6 | 100.4 | 486.6 | executive, operational, and security administration or management roles. |

| 48 | 227.3 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 201. The bank shall encourage the reporting of suspicious behaviour incidents to the |

| 48 | 248.0 | 100.4 | 248.5 | incident management team. |

| 48 | 280.7 | 72.0 | 477.0 | 202. The bank shall evaluate the awareness level of employees periodically. |

| 48 | 313.4 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 203. The bank shall establish a mechanism for continuous and adaptive capacity |

| 48 | 334.1 | 100.4 | 523.4 | building to strengthen cybersecurity management. Cybersecurity awareness |

| 48 | 354.8 | 100.4 | 523.3 | programmes shall be mandatory for all new recruits, and annual training shall be |

| 48 | 375.5 | 100.4 | 343.9 | conducted for lower and middle management. |

| 48 | 408.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 204. The bank shall ensure that the Board and Senior Management maintain |

| 48 | 428.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | adequate awareness of evolving cyber threats by periodically sensitising them to |

| 48 | 449.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | technological advancements and cybersecurity developments. The bank shall |

| 48 | 470.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | also provide annual training to all Board members and Senior Management on |

| 48 | 491.0 | 100.4 | 387.6 | IT and cybersecurity risks and evolving best practices. |

| 48 | 524.3 | 72.0 | 309.0 | CC. Customer Education and Awareness |

| 48 | 556.4 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 205. The bank shall improve and maintain customer awareness and education |

| 48 | 577.0 | 100.4 | 256.5 | regarding cybersecurity risks. |

| 48 | 609.7 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 206. The bank shall encourage customers to report phishing mails / phishing sites and |

| 48 | 630.4 | 100.4 | 435.7 | on such reporting, the bank shall take effective remedial action. |

| 48 | 663.1 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 207. The bank shall educate the customers about the risks and consequences of |

| 48 | 683.8 | 100.4 | 523.4 | sharing their login credentials, passwords, One-Time Passwords (OTPs), |

| 48 | 704.5 | 100.4 | 509.3 | Personal Identification Numbers (PINs), and other authentication information. |

| 48 | 737.2 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 208. The bank shall proactively promote awareness of its cyber resilience objectives |

| 48 | 757.9 | 100.4 | 199.1 | among customers. |

| 48 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 48 |

| 49 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 49 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 49 | 79.7 | 72.0 | 300.4 | DD. Risk based Transaction Monitoring |

| 49 | 111.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 209. The bank shall implement risk-based transaction monitoring or surveillance |

| 49 | 132.5 | 100.4 | 516.6 | process as part of fraud risk management system across all delivery channels. |

| 49 | 165.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 210. The bank shall notify the customer, through alternate communication channels, |

| 49 | 185.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | of all payment or fund transfer transactions above a specified value determined |

| 49 | 206.6 | 100.4 | 188.4 | by the customer. |

| 49 | 239.9 | 72.0 | 157.0 | EE. Forensics |

| 49 | 272.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 211. The bank shall have support / arrangement for network forensics / forensic |

| 49 | 292.7 | 100.4 | 375.6 | investigation / DDoS mitigation services on standby. |

| 49 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 49 |

| 50 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 50 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 50 | 79.7 | 166.0 | 429.4 | Chapter VI - Cyber Security Operations Centre |

| 50 | 112.4 | 72.0 | 169.8 | A. Governance |

| 50 | 144.5 | 72.0 | 523.4 | 212. The bank shall put in place a framework for establishment and operation of a |

| 50 | 165.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | Cyber Security Operations Centre (CSOC), commensurate with its technology |

| 50 | 185.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | risk profile, scale and complexity of operations, business requirements and |

| 50 | 206.6 | 100.4 | 327.2 | applicable regulatory and legal obligations. |

| 50 | 239.3 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 213. The bank shall ensure appropriate governance arrangements while setting up |

| 50 | 260.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | the CSOC, including briefing of the Board / ITSC and Senior Management on |

| 50 | 280.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | threat intelligence; establishment of dashboards for effective oversight; |

| 50 | 301.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | formulation and enforcement of policy with defined measurement and |

| 50 | 322.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | mechanisms such as key metrics and reporting structures specifying what is to |

| 50 | 342.8 | 100.4 | 441.9 | be reported; and timely communication with all the stakeholders. |

| 50 | 376.1 | 72.0 | 167.7 | B. Capabilities |

| 50 | 408.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 214. The CSOC shall ensure proactive monitoring and management capabilities with |

| 50 | 428.9 | 100.4 | 471.9 | sophisticated tools for detection, quick response, and sound analytics. |

| 50 | 461.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 215. The bank shall ensure that the systems implemented for monitoring security |

| 50 | 482.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | operations shall enable collection of logs from deployed systems / products and |

| 50 | 503.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | of various network activities; storage and processing of such logs; correlation of |

| 50 | 523.7 | 100.4 | 523.4 | logs through appropriate SIEM tools; continuous monitoring of SIEM dashboards |

| 50 | 544.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | to identify anomalies, if any; and generation of alerts / events / incidents to |

| 50 | 565.0 | 100.4 | 345.8 | escalate any abnormal / undesirable activities. |

| 50 | 597.7 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 216. The bank shall ensure that the systems as part of CSOC addresses the following: |

| 50 | 630.4 | 100.3 | 346.2 | (1) identification of the root cause of attacks; |

| 50 | 663.1 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (2) classification of such attacks into identified categories, and implementation |

| 50 | 683.8 | 128.7 | 416.2 | of solutions to contain further attacks of similar nature; |

| 50 | 716.5 | 100.3 | 466.8 | (3) capabilities for incident investigation and problem management; |

| 50 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 50 |

| 51 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 51 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 51 | 79.1 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (4) dynamic behaviour analysis (including preliminary static and dynamic |

| 51 | 99.8 | 128.7 | 179.4 | analysis); |

| 51 | 132.5 | 100.3 | 374.1 | (5) collection of Indicators of Compromise (IOCs); |

| 51 | 165.2 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (6) analytics supported by effective dashboards, including visibility of IP geo- |

| 51 | 185.9 | 128.7 | 387.4 | location and counter-response mechanisms; and |

| 51 | 218.6 | 100.3 | 228.7 | (7) honeypot services. |

| 51 | 251.3 | 72.0 | 447.2 | 217. The bank shall ensure that CSOC is responsible for the following: |

| 51 | 284.0 | 100.3 | 421.4 | (1) monitoring, analysing and escalating security incidents; |

| 51 | 316.7 | 100.3 | 523.2 | (2) developing and executing response measures across the protect, detect, |

| 51 | 337.4 | 128.7 | 287.4 | respond, and recover phases; |

| 51 | 370.1 | 100.3 | 446.9 | (3) conducting incident management and forensic analysis; and |

| 51 | 402.8 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (4) coordinating with relevant contact groups within the bank as well as with |

| 51 | 423.5 | 128.7 | 292.8 | external agencies, as required. |

| 51 | 456.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 218. The CSOC shall have security analytics engine which can process the logs within |

| 51 | 476.9 | 100.4 | 523.4 | reasonable time frame and come out with possible recommendations with |

| 51 | 497.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | options for further deep dive investigations and deliver wire speed performance |

| 51 | 518.3 | 100.4 | 304.5 | with on-the-fly deep packet inspection. |

| 51 | 551.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 219. The CSOC shall include tools and technologies for malware detection and |

| 51 | 571.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | analysis as well as imaging solutions for data to address the forensics |

| 51 | 592.4 | 100.4 | 173.8 | requirements. |

| 51 | 625.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 220. The bank shall ensure that the CSOC’s solution architecture deployed addresses |

| 51 | 645.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | the performance and scalability requirements in addition to high availability and |

| 51 | 666.4 | 100.4 | 167.7 | is capable of |

| 51 | 699.1 | 100.3 | 523.2 | (1) protecting critical business and customer data and information, |

| 51 | 719.8 | 128.7 | 523.3 | demonstrating compliance with internal guidelines as well as applicable |

| 51 | 740.5 | 128.7 | 241.4 | regulations and laws; |

| 51 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 51 |

| 52 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 52 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 52 | 79.1 | 100.3 | 523.4 | (2) providing real-time or near real-time information and insights into the |

| 52 | 99.8 | 128.7 | 279.5 | security posture of the bank; |

| 52 | 132.5 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (3) effectively and efficiently managing security operations by preparing for and |

| 52 | 153.2 | 128.7 | 523.3 | responding to cyber risks and threats and facilitating business continuity |

| 52 | 173.9 | 128.7 | 201.5 | and recovery; |

| 52 | 206.6 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (4) assessing threat intelligence and proactively identifying and visualising the |

| 52 | 227.3 | 128.7 | 286.8 | impact of threats on the bank; |

| 52 | 260.0 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (5) establishing clear attribution of actions including who did what, when and |

| 52 | 280.7 | 128.7 | 371.5 | how, along with preservation of evidence; and |

| 52 | 313.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (6) integrating various log types and logging options into SIEM and related |

| 52 | 334.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | systems, including ticketing, workflow and case management, unstructured |

| 52 | 354.8 | 128.7 | 523.3 | and big data platforms, and development of use cases and rules |

| 52 | 375.5 | 128.7 | 509.3 | customised to the bank’s risk and compliance requirements and drivers. |

| 52 | 408.8 | 72.0 | 197.8 | C. Staff Capabilities |

| 52 | 440.9 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 221. The bank shall put in place an appropriate organisational structure to ensure that |

| 52 | 461.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | the CSOC is managed and monitored on a continuous basis by competent and |

| 52 | 482.3 | 100.4 | 523.4 | capable personnel. All personnel engaged in CSOC operations shall possess |

| 52 | 503.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | adequate knowledge of the bank’s products, services, and deployed technology |

| 52 | 523.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | environment. The structure shall provide for role-based responsibilities across |

| 52 | 544.4 | 100.4 | 377.6 | different levels of operations, including the following: |

| 52 | 577.0 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (1) Round-the-clock Level 1 monitoring by adequately trained personnel with |

| 52 | 597.7 | 128.7 | 351.4 | relevant product and vendor certifications; |

| 52 | 630.4 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (2) Level 2 capabilities staffed by personnel with specialised expertise in areas |

| 52 | 651.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | such as network, data, and endpoint security to support root cause analysis |

| 52 | 671.8 | 128.7 | 356.2 | and to take suitable corrective actions; and |

| 52 | 704.5 | 100.3 | 523.3 | (3) Level 3 capabilities comprising SOC analysts with advanced security |

| 52 | 725.2 | 128.7 | 523.3 | expertise, including deep packet analysis, collection of IOCs, forensic |

| 52 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 52 |

| 53 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 53 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 53 | 79.1 | 128.7 | 523.3 | knowledge for collection of evidence, malware reverse engineering and |

| 53 | 99.8 | 128.7 | 360.8 | development of custom scripts, as required. |

| 53 | 132.5 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 222. The bank shall adopt a differentiated and structured approach to hiring, |

| 53 | 153.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | managing, and retaining personnel for the CSOC, recognising the specialised |

| 53 | 173.9 | 100.4 | 216.5 | nature of the function. |

| 53 | 206.6 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 223. The bank shall put in place appropriate mechanisms to determine staffing |

| 53 | 227.3 | 100.4 | 523.3 | requirements for continuous monitoring on 24x7 basis. The bank shall adopt |

| 53 | 248.0 | 100.4 | 523.3 | suitable sourcing and operating models including in-house staffing or managed |

| 53 | 268.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | service arrangements, ensure adequate training of personnel whether internal or |

| 53 | 289.4 | 100.4 | 523.3 | service provider-deployed, establish appropriate compensation and incentive |

| 53 | 310.1 | 100.4 | 523.3 | structures to retain skilled staff, define metrics to assess SOC performance, and |

| 53 | 330.8 | 100.4 | 523.4 | ensure scalability and continuity of personnel through effective capacity planning |

| 54 | 31.2 | 286.1 | 523.2 | RBI (CBs – Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 54 | 41.5 | 334.1 | 523.3 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 54 | 79.7 | 184.5 | 410.7 | Chapter VII - Information Systems Audit |

| 54 | 111.8 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 224. The Audit Committee of the Board (ACB) shall be responsible for exercising |

| 54 | 132.5 | 100.4 | 273.2 | oversight of IS Audit of the bank. |

| 54 | 165.2 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 225. The bank shall put in place an IS Audit Policy. The IS Audit Policy shall clearly |

| 54 | 185.9 | 100.4 | 523.4 | describe key aspects, including its mandate, purpose, authority, audit universe, |

| 54 | 206.6 | 100.4 | 523.3 | and periodicity of audit. The ACB shall approve the policy and review it at least |

| 54 | 227.3 | 100.4 | 147.5 | annually. |

| 54 | 260.0 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 226. The ACB shall review critical issues highlighted related to IT / information |

| 54 | 280.7 | 100.4 | 523.3 | security / cybersecurity and provide appropriate direction and guidance to the |

| 54 | 301.4 | 100.4 | 211.5 | bank’s Management. |

| 54 | 334.1 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 227. The bank shall have a separate IS Audit function or resources who possess |

| 54 | 354.8 | 100.4 | 523.2 | required professional skills and competence within the Internal Audit function. |

| 54 | 375.5 | 100.4 | 523.3 | Where the bank uses external resources for conducting IS audit in areas where |

| 54 | 396.2 | 100.4 | 523.3 | skills are lacking within the bank, the responsibility and accountability for such |

| 54 | 416.9 | 100.4 | 523.3 | external IS audits would continue to remain with the competent authority within |

| 54 | 437.6 | 100.4 | 219.1 | Internal Audit function. |

| 54 | 470.3 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 228. The bank shall carry out IS Audit planning by adopting a risk-based audit |

| 54 | 491.0 | 100.4 | 153.7 | approach. |

| 54 | 523.7 | 72.0 | 523.3 | 229. The bank may consider, wherever possible, a continuous auditing approach for |

| 54 | 544.4 | 100.4 | 523.2 | critical systems, performing control and risk assessments on a more frequent |

| 54 | 565.0 | 100.4 | 131.7 | basis. |

| 54 | 807.4 | 292.1 | 303.2 | 54 |

| 4 | TEXT |  | 78.6 | 72.0 | In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 27 and Section 35-A of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949, the Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934, and all other provisions / laws enabling the Reserve Bank of India ('RBI') in this regard, RBI being satisfied that it is necessary and expedient in the public interest so to do, hereby issues Directions hereinafter specified. |

| 4 | SECTION_HEADER | 1 | 194.1 | 233.6 | Chapter I - Preliminary |

| 4 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 226.8 | 73.0 | A. Short Title and Commencement |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 259.5 | 73.0 | These Directions shall be called the Reserve Bank of India (Commercial Banks - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026. |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 333.6 | 73.0 | These Directions shall come into effect immediately upon issuance. |

| 4 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 366.3 | 73.0 | B. Applicability |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 399.0 | 73.0 | These Directions shall be applicable to Commercial Banks (hereinafter collectively referred to as 'banks' and individually as a 'bank'). |

| 4 | TEXT |  | 452.4 | 100.4 | For the purpose of these Directions, 'Commercial Banks' means banking companies (other than Small Finance Banks, Payments Banks, and Local Area Banks), corresponding new banks, and the State Bank of India, as defined respectively under clauses (c), (da), and (nc) of Section 5 of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949. |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 567.9 | 73.0 | In the case of a foreign bank operating in India through branch mode, reference to the Board or Board of Directors in these Directions should be read as reference to the controlling office / head office which has the oversight over the branch operations in India. Further, such foreign bank shall be subject to a 'comply or explain' approach in terms of the applicability of Chapter II, Chapter III, Chapter IV, and Chapter VII and for the following select paragraphs in Chapter V of these Directions: |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 724.7 | 100.3 | Inventory Management of Information Assets - Paragraph 49 |

| 4 | LIST_ITEM |  | 757.4 | 100.3 | Data Migration Controls - Paragraph 55 |

| 5 | PICTURE |  | 24.9 | 19.4 | [picture element] |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 111.3 | 100.3 | Capacity Management - Paragraphs 64 and 65 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 144.0 | 100.3 | Application Security Life Cycle - Paragraphs 89, 90, 92 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 176.7 | 100.3 | Maintenance, Monitoring, and Analysis of Audit Logs - Paragraphs 93 and 95 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 230.1 | 100.3 | Patch, Vulnerability and Change Management - Paragraph 98 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 262.8 | 100.3 | User Access Control / Management - Paragraphs 104, 105 and 110 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 295.5 | 100.3 | Controls on Teleworking - Paragraph 114 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 328.2 | 100.3 | Third-Party Arrangements - Paragraph 126 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 360.9 | 100.3 | Cryptographic Controls - Paragraph 140 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 393.6 | 100.3 | Straight Through Processing - Paragraphs 141 and 142 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 426.3 | 100.3 | Vulnerability Assessment (VA) and Penetration Test (PT) - Paragraphs 151 to 155 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 479.7 | 100.3 | Business Continuity (BCP) and Disaster Recovery (DR) - Paragraphs 163 to 174 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 533.1 | 100.3 | Cyber Incident Response and Recovery Management - Paragraphs 175, 181, 183, 187, and 190 |

| 5 | LIST_ITEM |  | 586.5 | 100.3 | Metrics - Paragraphs 195 and 196 |

| 5 | TEXT |  | 619.2 | 100.4 | The 'comply or explain' approach shall allow such foreign bank to deviate from any specific part of the above referred Chapters and select paragraphs of Chapter V of these Directions subject to examination and acceptance by RBI of a reasonably justifiable explanation for the same, as part of the supervisory process. |

| 6 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.4 | [picture element] |

| 6 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 78.6 | 73.0 | C. Definitions |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 111.3 | 73.0 | The following definitions are sourced from FSB Cyber Lexicon unless explicitly mentioned otherwise. In these Directions, unless the context states otherwise, the terms herein shall bear the meanings assigned to them below. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 185.4 | 100.3 | 'Audit Trail' - A chronological record that reconstructs and examines the sequence of activities surrounding or leading to a specific operation, procedure, or event in a security-relevant transaction from inception to result. |

| 6 | TEXT |  | 280.2 | 128.7 | (Source: NIST SP 800-53r5 on Security and Privacy Controls for Information Systems and Organizations) |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 333.6 | 100.3 | 'Availability' - Property of being accessible and usable on demand by an authorised entity. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 387.0 | 100.3 | 'Confidentiality' - Property that information is neither made available nor disclosed to unauthorised individuals, entities, processes, or systems. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 440.4 | 100.3 | 'Cyber' - Relating to, within, or through the medium of the interconnected information infrastructure of interactions among persons, processes, data, and information systems. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 514.5 | 100.3 | 'Cyber Event' - Any observable occurrence in an information system. Cyber events sometimes provide indication that a cyber incident is occurring. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 567.9 | 100.3 | 'Cybersecurity' - Preservation of confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information and / or information systems through the cyber medium. In addition, other properties, such as authenticity, accountability, nonrepudiation, and reliability can also be involved. |

| 6 | LIST_ITEM |  | 662.6 | 100.3 | 'Cyber Incident' - A cyber event that adversely affects the cybersecurity of an information asset whether resulting from malicious activity or not. |

| 6 | TEXT |  | 716.0 | 128.7 | (Source: Cyber incident definition is adapted from FSB Cyber Lexicon. By the definition, it includes cybersecurity incidents as well as IT incidents) |

| 6 | TEXT |  | 30.3 | 286.1 | RBI (CBs - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience RBI (CBs - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 7 | PICTURE |  | 24.7 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | (8) 'Cyber Resilience' - The ability of an organisation to continue to carry out |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 99.3 | 128.7 | its mission by anticipating and adapting to cyber threats and other relevant |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 120.0 | 128.7 | changes in the environment and by withstanding, containing, and rapidly |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 140.7 | 128.7 | recovering from cyber incidents. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 173.4 | 100.3 | (9) 'Cyber-attack' - Malicious attempt(s) to exploit vulnerabilities through the |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 194.1 | 128.7 | cyber medium to damage, disrupt, or gain unauthorised access to assets. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 226.8 | 100.3 | (10) 'Cyber Threat' - A circumstance with the potential to exploit one or more |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 247.5 | 128.7 | vulnerabilities that adversely affects cybersecurity. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 280.2 | 100.3 | (11) 'Data Dictionary' - A description of data in business terms, including |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 300.9 | 128.7 | information about the data. It includes elements like data types, structure |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 321.6 | 128.7 | details, and security restrictions. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 354.3 | 128.7 | (Source: ISACA glossary) |

| 7 | LIST_ITEM |  | 387.0 | 100.3 | 'De-militarized Zone' or 'DMZ' - A perimeter network segment that is logically between internal and external networks. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 440.4 | 128.7 | (Source: NIST SP 800-82 Rev. 2) |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 473.1 | 100.3 | (13) 'Distributed Denial of Service (DDoS)' - A denial of service that is carried |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 493.8 | 128.7 | out using numerous sources simultaneously. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 526.5 | 100.3 | (14) 'Digital Forensics' - The process used to acquire, preserve, analyse, and |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 547.2 | 128.7 | report on evidence using scientific methods that are demonstrably reliable, |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 567.9 | 128.7 | accurate, and repeatable. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 600.5 | 128.7 | (Source: adapted from NIST Cloud Computing Forensic Science Challenges) |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 653.9 | 100.3 | (15) 'Framework' - A structured set of strategies, policies, processes, methods, |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 674.6 | 128.7 | and best practices that guides organisational activities, enables |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 695.3 | 128.7 | governance and control, and supports the achievement of defined |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 716.0 | 128.7 | objectives. |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 748.7 | 128.7 | (Source: adapted from ISACA glossary and ISO 22340:2024) |

| 7 | TEXT |  | 807.0 | 294.8 | 7 |

| 8 | PICTURE |  | 24.7 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 30.3 | 286.1 | RBI (CBs - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 40.7 | 334.1 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | (16) 'Honeypot' - A specially configured server, also known as a decoy server, |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 99.3 | 128.7 | designed to attract and monitor intruders in a manner so that their actions |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 120.0 | 128.7 | do not affect production systems. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 152.7 | 128.7 | (Source: ISACA glossary) |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 185.4 | 100.3 | (17) 'Indicators of Compromise (IOCs)' - Identifying signs that a cyber incident |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 206.1 | 128.7 | may have occurred or may be currently occurring. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 238.8 | 100.3 | (18) 'Information Asset' - Any piece of data, device, or other component of the |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 259.5 | 128.7 | environment that supports information-related activities. Information Assets |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 280.2 | 128.7 | include information system, data, hardware, and software. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 312.9 | 128.7 | (Source: Information Asset definition is adapted from "Guidance on cyber |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 333.6 | 128.7 | resilience for financial market infrastructures" publication of Bank for |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 354.3 | 128.7 | International Settlements and International Organization of Securities |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 375.0 | 128.7 | Commissions of June 2016) |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 407.7 | 100.3 | (19) 'Information System' - Set of applications, services, information technology |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 428.4 | 128.7 | assets, or other information-handling components, which includes the |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 449.1 | 128.7 | operating environment and networks. |

| 8 | LIST_ITEM |  | 481.8 | 100.3 | 'Integrity' - Property of accuracy and completeness. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 514.5 | 100.3 | (21) 'IT Governance' - The responsibility of executives and the board of |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 535.2 | 128.7 | directors; consists of the leadership, organisational structures, and |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 555.9 | 128.7 | processes that ensure that the enterprise's IT sustains and extends the |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 576.5 | 128.7 | enterprise's strategies and objectives. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 609.2 | 128.7 | (Source: ISACA glossary and COBIT) |

| 8 | LIST_ITEM |  | 641.9 | 100.3 | 'IT Risk' - The business risk associated with the use, ownership, operation, involvement, influence, and adoption of IT within an enterprise. |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 695.3 | 128.7 | (Source: ISACA glossary) |

| 8 | TEXT |  | 807.0 | 294.8 | 8 |

| 9 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 30.3 | 286.1 | RBI (CBs - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 40.7 | 334.1 | and Assurance Framework) Directions, 2026 |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | (23) 'Malware' - Software designed with malicious intent containing features or |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 99.3 | 128.7 | capabilities that can potentially cause harm directly or indirectly to entities |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 120.0 | 128.7 | or their information systems. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 152.7 | 100.3 | (24) 'Penetration Testing' - A test methodology in which assessors typically |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 173.4 | 128.7 | working under specific constraints, attempt to circumvent or defeat the |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 194.1 | 128.7 | security features of an information system. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 226.8 | 100.3 | (25) 'Phishing' - A digital form of social engineering that attempts to acquire |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 247.5 | 128.7 | private or confidential information by pretending to be a trustworthy entity |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 268.2 | 128.7 | in an electronic communication. |

| 9 | LIST_ITEM |  | 300.9 | 100.3 | 'Privileged User' - A user who, by virtue of function, and / or role, has been allocated powers within an information system, which are significantly greater than those available to the majority of users. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 375.0 | 128.7 | (Source: adapted from ISO/IEC 24775-2:2021) |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 407.7 | 100.3 | (27) 'Recovery Point Objective' - The point in time to which data must be |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 428.4 | 128.7 | recovered after an outage. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 461.1 | 128.7 | (Source: NIST glossary) |

| 9 | LIST_ITEM |  | 493.8 | 100.3 | 'Recovery Time Objective' - The overall length of time an information system's components can be in the recovery phase before negatively impacting the organisation's mission or mission / business processes. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 567.9 | 128.7 | (Source: NIST glossary) |

| 9 | LIST_ITEM |  | 600.5 | 100.3 | 'Red Team' - A group of people authorised and organised to emulate a potential adversary's attack or exploitation capabilities against an enterprise's security posture. The Red Team's objective is to improve enterprise cybersecurity by demonstrating the impacts of successful attacks and by demonstrating what works for the defenders (i.e., the Blue Team) in an operational environment. |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 736.7 | 128.7 | (Source: NIST glossary) |

| 9 | TEXT |  | 807.0 | 294.8 | 9 |

| 10 | PICTURE |  | 24.7 | 19.2 | [picture element] |

| 10 | TEXT |  | 30.3 | 286.1 | RBI (CBs - Cybersecurity, Technology: Risk, Resilience |

| 10 | LIST_ITEM |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | 'Red Teaming Exercise' - An exercise, reflecting real-world conditions, that is conducted as a simulated adversarial attempt by a red team to compromise organisational missions and / or business processes to provide a comprehensive assessment of the security capability of the information system and organisation. |

| 10 | TEXT |  | 194.1 | 128.7 | (Source: adapted from NIST glossary) |

| 10 | LIST_ITEM |  | 226.8 | 100.3 | 'Vulnerability' - A weakness, susceptibility, or flaw of an asset or control that can be exploited by one or more threats. |

| 10 | LIST_ITEM |  | 280.2 | 100.3 | 'Vulnerability Assessment (VA)' - Systematic examination of an information system or product to determine the adequacy of security measures, identify security deficiencies, provide data from which to predict the effectiveness of proposed security measures and confirm the adequacy of such measures after implementation. |

| 10 | LIST_ITEM |  | 395.7 | 63.0 | All other expressions unless defined herein shall have the same meaning as have been assigned to them under the Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934, the Banking Regulation Act, 1949, the Information Technology Act, 2000, the Companies Act, 2013 or any statutory modification or re-enactment thereto or other regulations issued by the RBI or the Glossary of Terms published by the RBI or as used in commercial parlance, as the case may be. |

| 11 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 11 | SECTION_HEADER | 1 | 78.6 | 214.6 | Chapter II - Role of the Board |

| 11 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 111.3 | 73.0 | A. Board Approved Policies |

| 11 | LIST_ITEM |  | 144.0 | 72.0 | The Board of Directors shall approve the strategies and policies related to Information Technology, Information Assets, Business Continuity, Information Security and Cybersecurity (including Incident Response and Recovery Management / Cyber Crisis Management) frameworks. |

| 11 | LIST_ITEM |  | 238.8 | 72.0 | The Board may delegate the review of such policies and strategies to the Boardlevel IT Strategy Committee (ITSC). Material amendments thereto shall be approved by the Board. The review shall be undertaken at least annually 1 . |

| 11 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 312.9 | 73.0 | B. Board Level Committees |

| 11 | LIST_ITEM |  | 345.6 | 72.0 | The bank shall put in place a Board-level IT Strategy Committee (ITSC). The composition and functions of the Committee shall be in accordance with paragraphs 16 to 19 of Chapter III. |

| 11 | LIST_ITEM |  | 419.7 | 72.0 | The Audit Committee of the Board (ACB) shall be responsible for exercising oversight of IS Audit of the bank. |

| 11 | FOOTNOTE |  | 748.2 | 72.0 | 1 Substituted w.e.f. October 1, 2026, consequent to Reserve Bank of India (Commercial Banks - Governance) Amendment Directions, 2026. |

| 12 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 12 | SECTION_HEADER | 1 | 78.6 | 115.4 | Chapter III - Information Technology Governance and Oversight |

| 12 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 111.3 | 72.0 | A. IT Governance Framework |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 144.0 | 72.0 | The key focus areas of IT Governance shall include strategic alignment, risk management, resource management, performance management, and Business Continuity / DR Management. |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 218.1 | 72.0 | The bank shall put in place a robust IT Governance Framework based on the focus areas outlined in Paragraph 11 above, that inter alia: |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 271.5 | 100.3 | specifies the governance structure and processes necessary to meet the bank's business / strategic objectives; |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 324.9 | 100.3 | specifies the roles (including authority) and responsibilities of the Board of Directors (Board) / Board level Committee and Senior Management; and |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 378.3 | 100.3 | includes adequate oversight mechanisms to ensure accountability and mitigation of IT and cyber / information security risks. |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 431.7 | 72.0 | Enterprise-wide risk management policy or operational risk management policy shall also incorporate periodic assessment of IT-related risks (both inherent and potential risk). |

| 12 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 505.8 | 72.0 | B. Information Security Policy and Cybersecurity Policy |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 538.5 | 72.0 | The bank shall put in place an Information Security Policy that takes into consideration, inter alia, aspects such as the objectives, scope, ownership, and responsibility for the policy; information security organisational structure; exceptions; compliance review and penal measures for non-compliance of policies. |

| 12 | LIST_ITEM |  | 653.9 | 72.0 | The bank shall put in place a Cybersecurity Policy elucidating the strategy containing an appropriate approach to combat cyber threats given the level of complexity of business and acceptable levels of risk, duly approved by its Board. The cybersecurity policy shall be distinct and separate from the broader IT policy / Information Security policy so that it can highlight the risks from cyber threats and the measures to address / mitigate these risks. |

| 13 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 13 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 78.6 | 72.0 | C. IT Strategy Committee of the Board |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 111.3 | 72.0 | The bank shall establish a Board-level IT Strategy Committee (ITSC). |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 144.0 | 72.0 | While constituting the ITSC, the bank shall ensure the following - |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 176.7 | 100.3 | ITSC has minimum of three directors (including the Chairperson of the ITSC) as members; |

| 13 | TEXT |  | 230.1 | 128.7 | (Note: Foreign banks operating in India through branch mode may leverage upon controlling office / head office / regional / zonal Committees for compliance with this Master Direction as long as governance obligations / responsibilities outlined for the prescribed committees are met.) |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 324.9 | 100.3 | The Chairperson of the ITSC is an independent director having substantial IT expertise in managing / guiding information technology initiatives; 'Substantial IT expertise' means having a minimum of seven years' experience in managing information systems and / or leading / guiding technology/ cybersecurity initiatives / projects. The Chairperson should also understand the business processes at a broader level and the impact of IT on such processes. |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 481.8 | 100.3 | Members are technically competent. Technically competent herein shall mean the ability to understand and evaluate information systems and associated IT / cyber risks. |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 555.9 | 72.0 | The ITSC shall meet at least on a quarterly basis. |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 588.5 | 72.0 | The ITSC shall: |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 621.2 | 100.3 | ensure that the bank has put an effective IT strategic planning process in place; |

| 13 | LIST_ITEM |  | 674.6 | 100.3 | guide in preparation of IT Strategy and ensure that the IT Strategy aligns with the overall strategy of the bank towards accomplishment of its business objectives; |

| 14 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | satisfy itself that the IT Governance and Information Security Governance structure fosters accountability, is effective and efficient, has adequate skilled resources, well - defined objectives, and unambiguous responsibilities for each level in the organisation; |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 173.4 | 100.3 | ensure that the bank has put in place processes for assessing and managing IT and cybersecurity risks; |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 226.8 | 100.3 | ensure that the budgetary allocations for the IT function, including IT security / cybersecurity, are commensurate with the bank's IT maturity, digital depth, threat environment, and industry standards and are utilised in a manner intended for meeting the stated objectives; |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 321.6 | 100.3 | review the organisational arrangements so that the security concerns are appreciated, receive adequate attention, and get escalated to appropriate levels in the hierarchy to enable quick action; |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 395.7 | 100.3 | review, at least on an annual basis, the adequacy and effectiveness of the Business Continuity Planning and Disaster Recovery (DR) Management of the bank; |

| 14 | TEXT |  | 469.8 | 128.7 | (Note: The reference to Business Continuity / Disaster Recovery Management in these Directions is limited to operational resilience focussing on People, Processes, and Systems associated with the IT, Information Systems (IS), information security / cybersecurity controls and operations) |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 585.2 | 100.3 | review the assessment of IT capacity requirements and the measures taken to address the issues; and |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 638.6 | 100.3 | approve documented standards and procedures for administering needbased access to an information system. |

| 14 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 692.0 | 72.0 | D. Senior Management and IT Steering Committee |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 724.7 | 72.0 | The Senior Management of the bank shall, inter alia, ensure: |

| 14 | LIST_ITEM |  | 757.4 | 100.3 | execution of the IT Strategy approved by the Board; |

| 15 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.2 | [picture element] |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | IT / Information Security and their support infrastructure are functioning effectively and efficiently; |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 132.0 | 100.3 | necessary IT risk management processes are in place and create a culture of IT risk awareness and cyber hygiene practices in the bank; |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 185.4 | 100.3 | cybersecurity posture of the bank is robust; and |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 218.1 | 100.3 | overall, IT contributes to productivity, effectiveness, and efficiency in business operations. |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 271.5 | 72.0 | The bank shall establish an IT Steering Committee with representation at Senior Management level from IT and business functions which shall meet at least on a quarterly basis. |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 345.6 | 72.0 | The responsibilities of IT Steering Committee, inter alia, shall be to: |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 378.3 | 100.3 | assist the ITSC in strategic IT planning, oversight of IT performance, and aligning IT activities with business needs; |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 431.7 | 100.3 | oversee the processes put in place for business continuity and DR; |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 464.4 | 100.3 | ensure implementation of a robust IT architecture meeting statutory and regulatory compliance; and |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 517.8 | 100.3 | update the ITSC and Managing Director (MD) / Chief Executive Officer (CEO) periodically on the activities of IT Steering Committee. |

| 15 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 571.2 | 72.0 | E. Information Security Committee |

| 15 | LIST_ITEM |  | 603.9 | 72.0 | An Information Security Committee (ISC), under the oversight of the ITSC, shall be formed for managing cyber / information security. The constitution of the ISC, with Chief Information Security Officer (CISO) and other representatives from business, IT, and other relevant functions, shall be decided by the ITSC. The head of the ISC shall be from risk management vertical. Major responsibilities of the ISC, inter alia, shall include: |

| 16 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.2 | [picture element] |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 78.6 | 100.3 | development of information security / cybersecurity policies, implementation of policies, standards, and procedures to ensure that all identified risks are managed within the bank's risk appetite; |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 152.7 | 100.3 | approving and monitoring information security / cybersecurity projects and security awareness initiatives; |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 206.1 | 100.3 | reviewing cyber incidents, IS audit observations, monitoring, and mitigation activities; and |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 259.5 | 100.3 | updating the ITSC and MD / CEO periodically on the activities of ISC. |

| 16 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 292.2 | 72.0 | F. Head of IT Function |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 324.9 | 72.0 | The bank shall appoint a sufficiently senior level, technically competent, and experienced official in IT related aspects as Head of IT Function (or by whatever nomenclature the bank uses, such as Chief Technology Officer, Chief Information Officer). |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 419.7 | 72.0 | The Head of IT Function shall, inter alia, be responsible for the following: |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 452.4 | 100.3 | ensuring that the execution of IT projects / initiatives is aligned with the bank's IT Policy and IT Strategy; |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 505.8 | 100.3 | ensuring that there is an effective organisational structure to support IT functions in the bank; and |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 559.2 | 100.3 | putting in place an effective DR setup and business continuity strategy / plan. |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 612.5 | 72.0 | As a first line of defence, the Head of IT Function shall ensure effective assessment, evaluation, and management of IT controls and IT risk, including the implementation of robust internal controls, to |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 686.6 | 100.3 | secure the bank's information assets; and |

| 16 | LIST_ITEM |  | 719.3 | 100.3 | comply with extant internal policies, regulatory and legal requirements on IT related aspects. |

| 17 | PICTURE |  | 24.8 | 19.3 | [picture element] |

| 17 | SECTION_HEADER | 3 | 78.6 | 72.0 | G. Chief Information Security Officer |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 111.3 | 72.0 | A senior level executive (preferably in the rank of a General Manager or an equivalent position) shall be designated as the CISO. The CISO shall not have any direct reporting relationship with the Head of IT Function and shall not be given any business targets. The bank shall ensure the following: |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 206.1 | 100.3 | The CISO has the requisite technical background and expertise. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 238.8 | 100.3 | The CISO is appointed for a reasonable minimum term. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 271.5 | 100.3 | The CISO's office is adequately staffed with people having necessary technical expertise, commensurate with the business volume, extent of technology adoption and complexity. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 345.6 | 100.3 | The budget for the information security / cybersecurity is determined keeping in view the current / emerging threat landscape. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 399.0 | 72.0 | The bank shall ensure that the roles and responsibilities of the CISO are clearly defined and documented covering, at a minimum, the following points: |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 452.4 | 100.3 | The CISO shall be responsible for driving cybersecurity strategy and ensuring compliance to the extant regulatory / statutory instructions on information security / cybersecurity. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 526.5 | 100.3 | The CISO shall be responsible for enforcing the policies that a bank uses to protect its information assets apart from coordinating information security / cybersecurity related issues within the bank as well as with relevant external agencies. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 621.2 | 100.3 | The CISO shall be a permanent invitee to the ITSC and IT Steering Committee. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 674.6 | 100.3 | The CISO's office shall manage and monitor Security Operations Centre (SOC) and drive cybersecurity related projects. |

| 17 | LIST_ITEM |  | 728.0 | 100.3 | The CISO's office shall ensure effective functioning of the security solutions deployed. |
